\left\lvert\varphi(r)-\mathring{\varphi}(r)\right\rvert\leq\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\left\lvert\chi-\mathring{\chi}\right\rvert}{s^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}s\leq\mathsf{g}w_{2}\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{2}p(s)^{-2}q(s)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mathsf{h}}e^{-s}\ \mathrm{d}s\leq\mathsf{g}w_{2}K_{11}q(r)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mathsf{h}}e^{-r},

proving (
4.157
) for the constant
C_{5}=w_{2}K_{11}
. The exact value of
\mathring{\varphi}(0)
when
\mathsf{h}=0
is
1=2\mathring{\mathcal{E}}
, while for
\mathsf{h}>0
it can be found by Taylor-expanding the
U_{\mathsf{h}}(4\mathsf{h}+2r)
and
e^{-r}
terms in (
4.161
) to a sufficiently high order in
r
:

\displaystyle\mathring{\varphi}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{2\mathsf{h}\,U_{\mathsf{h}}(4\mathsf{h})}\bigg(U_{\mathsf{h}}(4\mathsf{h})+2U_{\mathsf{h}}^{\prime}(4\mathsf{h})r+O(r^{2})\bigg)\left(1+\frac{2\mathsf{h}}{r}\right)\bigg(1-r+O(r^{2})\bigg)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{2\mathsf{h}}-\frac{2U_{\mathsf{h}}^{\prime}(4\mathsf{h})}{U_{\mathsf{h}}(4\mathsf{h})}+O(r)

\displaystyle=2\mathring{\mathcal{E}}+O(r)\quad\text{as }r\to 0,

where the definition (
4.17
) of
\mathring{\mathcal{E}}
was used. Therefore, for any
\mathsf{h}\geq 0
,

(4.163)

\mathring{\varphi}(0)=2\mathring{\mathcal{E}},

and now plugging
r=0
into (
4.162
) proves (
4.158
) with the constant
C_{6}=w_{2}K_{11}(1+4\mathsf{h})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mathsf{h}}
(which is further bounded by
w_{2}K_{11}5^{\frac{1}{2}}
when
0<\mathsf{h}<1
). We can also use (
4.160
) and estimate (
4.100
) for
\chi
to obtain a comparison between
\varphi
and the Coulomb potential
1/r
(note that this bound will not feature the constant
\mathsf{g}
, as it does not compare any function
f
with its
\mathsf{g}=0
counterpart
\mathring{f}
):

\left\lvert\varphi(r)-\frac{1}{r}\right\rvert=\left\lvert\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{1-\chi-1}{s^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}s\right\rvert\leq\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\left\lvert\chi\right\rvert}{s^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}s\leq W_{3}\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-2}q(s)^{\frac{3}{2}+\mathsf{h}}e^{-s}\ \mathrm{d}s\leq W_{3}K_{7}r^{-1}q(r)^{\frac{1}{2}+\mathsf{h}}e^{-r}.

This proves (
4.159
) for the constant
C_{4}=W_{3}K_{7}
.

•

Proof of (
4.26
)
. The desired inequality reads

(4.164)

\left\lvert M_{\mathrm{ADM}}-\big(M_{\mathrm{bare}}+\mathring{\mathcal{E}}\big)\right\rvert\leq\mathsf{g}C_{8}.

Start with the
\chi
equation as originally written in the E-M-BLTP system (
3.30
), but expressed in terms of
\nu
instead of
\beta
:

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\frac{(r-2\nu)\chi^{\prime}e^{\alpha}}{r^{3}}\right)=\frac{\chi e^{\alpha}}{r^{2}}.

Apply the
r
-derivative on the left side using the product rule for the factors
(r-2\nu)\chi^{\prime}/r^{3}
and
e^{\alpha}
, then use the
\alpha
equation to compute
(e^{\alpha})^{\prime}
and cancel
e^{\alpha}
from the whole equation to find

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\frac{(r-2\nu)\chi^{\prime}}{r^{3}}\right)-\mathsf{g}\frac{(r-2\nu)(\chi^{\prime})^{3}}{r^{6}}=\frac{\chi}{r^{2}}.

Add and subtract
1/r^{2}
to the right side, multiply the whole equation by
\chi-1
, and rearrange some terms:

\frac{1-\chi^{2}}{r^{2}}+(\chi-1)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\frac{(r-2\nu)\chi^{\prime}}{r^{3}}\right)=\frac{1-\chi}{r^{2}}+\mathsf{g}\frac{(r-2\nu)(\chi-1)(\chi^{\prime})^{3}}{r^{6}}.

Now integrate from 0 to
\infty
, using integration by parts on the second term on the left side:

(4.165)

\frac{(r-2\nu)(\chi-1)\chi^{\prime}}{r^{3}}\bigg|_{r=0}^{\infty}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1-\chi^{2}}{r^{2}}-\frac{(r-2\nu)(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{r^{3}}\right)\ \mathrm{d}r\\
=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1-\chi}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r+\mathsf{g}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(r-2\nu)(\chi-1)(\chi^{\prime})^{3}}{r^{6}}\ \mathrm{d}r.

Here the first term on the left vanishes at both endpoints as a consequence of the bounds (
4.135
), (
4.99
), and (
4.101
) for
r-2\nu
,
\chi-1
and
\chi^{\prime}
, which imply that
(r-2\nu)(\chi-1)\chi^{\prime}/r^{3}
behaves like
r^{2}p(r)^{-1}q(r)^{-3/2+\mathsf{h}}e^{-r}
. Moreover, the integrand in the second term is exactly
2\nu^{\prime}(r)/\mathsf{g}=2\mu^{\prime}(r)
according to the
\nu
equation in (
4.41
) and the definition (
4.37
) of
\nu
in terms of
\mu
, while the first integral on the right is exactly
\varphi(0)
according to (
4.160
). Seeing as how
\mu(0)=M_{\mathrm{bare}}
and
\mu(\infty)=M_{\mathrm{ADM}}
, we have thus proved

(4.166)

2M_{\mathrm{ADM}}-2M_{\mathrm{bare}}=\varphi(0)+\mathsf{g}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(r-2\nu)(\chi-1)(\chi^{\prime})^{3}}{r^{6}}\ \mathrm{d}r.

Using the bounds mentioned just above for
r-2\nu
,
\chi-1
and
\chi^{\prime}
, we estimate this integral as

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(r-2\nu)(\chi-1)(\chi^{\prime})^{3}}{r^{6}}\ \mathrm{d}r\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3W_{2}W_{4}^{3}}{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{3}p(r)^{-3}q(r)^{-\frac{1}{2}+3\mathsf{h}}e^{-3r}\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3W_{2}W_{4}^{3}}{32}\int_{0}^{\infty}q(r)^{-\frac{1}{2}+3\mathsf{h}}e^{-3r}\ \mathrm{d}r.

Meanwhile, the
\varphi(0)
term is approximately given by
2\mathring{\mathcal{E}}
with error bound
\mathsf{g}C_{6}
given in (
4.158
). With this, estimate (
4.166
) implies (
4.164
) with the constant

C_{8}=\frac{1}{2}\left(C_{6}+\frac{3W_{2}W_{4}^{3}}{32}\int_{0}^{\infty}q(r)^{-\frac{1}{2}+3\mathsf{h}}e^{-3r}\ \mathrm{d}r\right).

The numerical values (
4.27
) of
C_{8}
in the case
0\leq\mathsf{h}<1
are derived directly from here by first bounding this integral with computer help: For
\mathsf{h}=0
, the integral can be directly calculated to be less than
0.280
; for
0<\mathsf{h}<1
, it can be estimated above by its value at
\mathsf{h}=1
, that comes out smaller than
26.2
.

•

Proof of (
4.25
)
. The desired inequality reads

(4.167)

\left\lvert\mathcal{E}-\mathring{\mathcal{E}}\right\rvert\leq\mathsf{g}C_{7},

Reasoning similarly to how we did in the previous item, but this time not canceling any
e^{\alpha}
terms, we prove an equation similar to (
4.165
):

\frac{e^{\alpha}(r-2\nu)(\chi-1)\chi^{\prime}}{r^{3}}\bigg|_{r=0}^{\infty}+\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\alpha}\left(\frac{1-\chi^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{(r-2\nu)(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{r^{3}}\right)\ \mathrm{d}r=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{\alpha}(1-\chi)}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r.

Here one checks again that the first term vanishes at both endpoints, this time also making use of the known behavior of
e^{\alpha}
— in particular note that the already proved rate of divergence of
e^{\alpha}
around
r=0
when
\mathsf{h}=0
, given in (
4.155
), is not fast enough to become a problem, since
\delta<1
. Moreover, according to definition (
4.13
) of the field energy, we recognize
2\mathcal{E}
in the second term. This means that we have proved a simpler formula for it:

\mathcal{E}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{\alpha}(1-\chi)}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r.

We also remark that (
4.161
) and (
4.163
) together imply that the constant
\mathring{\mathcal{E}}
as defined in the theorem in (
4.17
) is the value of this expression in the
\mathsf{g}=0
case, that is, with
\alpha
and
\chi
replaced by
\mathring{\alpha}=0
and
\mathring{\chi}
. Indeed:

(4.168)

\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1-\mathring{\chi}}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r=\frac{\mathring{\varphi}(0)}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{2\mathsf{h}}-\frac{2U_{\mathsf{h}}^{\prime}(4\mathsf{h})}{U_{\mathsf{h}}(4\mathsf{h})}\right)=\mathring{\mathcal{E}}.

Consequently, we can compare
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathring{\mathcal{E}}
as follows:

(4.169)

\left\lvert\mathcal{E}-\mathring{\mathcal{E}}\right\rvert=\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{e^{\alpha}(1-\chi)}{r^{2}}-\frac{1-\mathring{\chi}}{r^{2}}\right)\ \mathrm{d}r\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(e^{\alpha}-1)(1-\chi)}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\left\lvert\mathring{\chi}-\chi\right\rvert}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r.

Inequality (
4.162
) with
r=0
provides a bound for the second integral on the right:

(4.170)

\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\left\lvert\mathring{\chi}-\chi\right\rvert}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}w_{2}K_{11}}{2}(1+4\mathsf{h})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mathsf{h}}=\frac{\mathsf{g}C_{6}}{2}.

As for the first integral, it is necessary to consider the cases
\mathsf{h}=0
and
\mathsf{h}>0
separately. If
\mathsf{h}=0
, estimates (
4.155
) and (
4.99
) for
e^{\alpha}-1
and
1-\chi
yield

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(e^{\alpha}-1)(1-\chi)}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-2}\mathsf{g}C_{1}\ln\left(e+\frac{e}{2r}\right)\left(e+\frac{e}{2r}\right)^{\delta}e^{-2r}W_{2}r^{3}p(r)^{-1}q(r)^{-2}\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathsf{g}W_{2}C_{1}}{4}\left(\frac{e}{2}\right)^{\delta}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-\delta}(1+2r)^{-2+\delta}\ln\left(e+\frac{e}{2r}\right)e^{-2r}\ \mathrm{d}r.

Using
\delta<0.0004
(note that
r^{-\delta}(1+2r)^{-2+\delta}
is increasing in
\delta
), a computer will estimate this integral as less than
0.553
. Using also the values of
W_{2}
,
C_{1}
and
C_{6}
corresponding to
\mathsf{h}=0
, estimate (
4.169
) gives the value of
C_{7}
as claimed in (
4.27
) for this case. If
\mathsf{h}>0
, estimates (
4.155
) and (
4.99
) for
e^{\alpha}-1
and
1-\chi
yield

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(e^{\alpha}-1)(1-\chi)}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-2}\mathsf{g}C_{1}\exp(C_1'/\sfh^2)p(r)^{-2}q(r)^{2+2\mathsf{h}}e^{-2r}W_{2}r^{3}p(r)^{-1}q(r)^{-2}\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathsf{g}W_{2}C_{1}\exp(C_1'/\sfh^2)}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}rp(r)^{-3}q(r)^{2n}e^{-2r}\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}W_{2}C_{1}\exp(C_1'/\sfh^2)}{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}p(r)^{-2}q(r)^{2n}e^{-2r}\ \mathrm{d}r.

Together with (
4.169
), this provides the general value

C_{7}=\frac{C_{6}}{2}+\frac{W_{2}C_{1}\exp(C_1'/\sfh^2)}{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}p(r)^{-2}q(r)^{2n}e^{-2r}\ \mathrm{d}r,\quad\mathsf{h}>0.

For small
\mathsf{h}
, however, this is far from being useful, since the
\exp(C_1'/\sfh^2)
term becomes large. We present now a different estimate, valid for
0<\mathsf{h}<1
, that yields a uniformly bounded value of
C_{7}
. The idea is to use a different estimate for
e^{\alpha}-1
, one which lets go of the finiteness at small
r
present in (
4.155
) in exchange for a bounded-in-
\mathsf{h}
constant. We go back to (
4.156
) and this time use
sp(s)^{-2}\leq s^{-1}/4
and
q(s)^{1+2\mathsf{h}}\leq(5+2s)^{3}
to find

\alpha(r)\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}W_{4}^{2}}{4}\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-1}(5+2s)^{3}e^{-2s}\ \mathrm{d}s=\frac{\mathsf{g}W_{4}^{2}}{4}\bigg(2(2r^{2}+17r+46)e^{-2r}+125E_{1}(2r)\bigg)\\
\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}W_{4}^{2}}{4}\bigg(2(2r^{2}+17r+46)+125\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)\bigg)e^{-2r}.

In particular, using the values of
\mathsf{g}<\mathsf{g}_{0}
and
W_{4}
for the
0<\mathsf{h}<1
case,

e^{\alpha(r)}\leq\Bigg(\exp\Big( 2(2r^2+17r+46)e^{-2r} + 125e^{-2r}\ln\big(1+\frac{1}{2r}\big) \Big)\Bigg)^{\mathsf{g}W_{4}^{2}/4}\leq\bigg(e^{92}\big(1+\frac{1}{2r}\big)^{125}\bigg)^{\mathsf{g}W_{4}^{2}/4}\\
<1.01\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)^{5.63\cdot 10^{-14}},

so that the inequality
e^{\alpha}-1\leq\alpha e^{\alpha}
now yields

e^{\alpha(r)}-1\leq 38.9\mathsf{g}\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)^{\delta}\bigg(2(2r^{2}+17r+46)+125\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)\bigg)e^{-2r}

for the small exponent
\delta=5.63\cdot 10^{-14}
. We plug this into the first integral on the right side of (
4.169
) and again use (
4.170
) on the second integral:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{E}-\mathring{\mathcal{E}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(e^{\alpha}-1)(1-\chi)}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\left\lvert\mathring{\chi}-\chi\right\rvert}{r^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{38.9\mathsf{g}W_{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{-2}\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)^{\delta}\bigg(2(2r^{2}+17r+46)+125\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)\bigg)e^{-2r}r^{3}p^{-1}q^{-2}\ \mathrm{d}r+\frac{\mathsf{g}C_{6}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{38.9\mathsf{g}W_{2}}{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)^{\delta}\bigg(2(2r^{2}+17r+46)+125\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)\bigg)e^{-2r}\frac{1}{(1+2r)^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r+\frac{\mathsf{g}C_{6}}{2},

where
rp^{-1}q^{-2}
was ultimately bounded by
(1/2)(1+2r)^{-2}
to get rid of all dependency on
\mathsf{h}
. This proves (
4.167
) in the
0<\mathsf{h}<1
case for the constant

C_{7}=\frac{38.9W_{2}}{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)^{5.63\cdot 10^{-14}}\bigg(2(2r^{2}+17r+46)+125\ln\left(1+\frac{1}{2r}\right)\bigg)\frac{e^{-2r}}{(1+2r)^{2}}\ \mathrm{d}r+\frac{C_{6}}{2}<2.91\cdot 10^{16}.

•

Proof of (
4.21
) and (
4.20
)
. The desired inequalities read

(4.171)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\beta(r)-\left(1+\frac{2\mathsf{h}}{r}\right)^{-1}\right\rvert\leq\mathsf{g}C_{3}r^{3}p(r)^{-3}q(r)^{-1},

(4.172)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\beta(r)-\beta^{\mathrm{(RWN)}}_{M_{\mathrm{ADM}}}(r)\right\rvert\leq\mathsf{g}C_{2}rp(r)^{-1}q(r)^{2+2\mathsf{h}}e^{-2r}\beta^{\mathrm{(RWN)}}_{M_{\mathrm{ADM}}}(r).

Comparing
\beta=r/(r-2\nu)=r/(r+2\mathsf{h}-2\mathsf{g}\xi)
to its counterpart in the
\mathsf{g}=0
case, which is
r/(r-2\mathring{\nu})=r/(r+2\mathsf{h})
, and using the estimate (
4.102
) for
\nu-\mathring{\nu}=\mathsf{g}\xi
and the last inequality in (
4.135
), namely
1/(r+2\mathsf{h}-2\mathsf{g}\xi)\leq 4p(r)^{-1}
, we immediately derive

(4.173)

\left\lvert\beta-\frac{r}{r+2\mathsf{h}}\right\rvert=\frac{2\mathsf{g}r\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert}{(r+2\mathsf{h})(r+2\mathsf{h}-2\mathsf{g}\xi)}\leq 16\mathsf{g}w_{1}r^{3}p(r)^{-3}q(r)^{-1},

proving (
4.171
) for the constant
C_{3}=16w_{1}
. In turn, comparing
\beta
to the RWN coefficient

\beta^{\text{(RWN)}}_{M}=\left(1-\frac{2\mathsf{g}M}{r}+\frac{\mathsf{g}}{r^{2}}\right)^{-1}=\frac{r^{2}}{r^{2}-2\nu(\infty)r+\mathsf{g}}

defined in (
3.33
), with the finite parameter
M=M_{\mathrm{ADM}}=\nu(\infty)/\mathsf{g}
, we find

\beta-\beta^{\mathrm{(RWN)}}_{M}=\frac{r}{r-2\nu}-\frac{r^{2}}{r^{2}-2\nu(\infty)r+\mathsf{g}}=\frac{2\beta^{\mathrm{(RWN)}}_{M}}{r+2\mathsf{h}-2\mathsf{g}\xi}\left(\nu-\nu(\infty)+\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2r}\right).

Now this gives us the estimate

(4.174)

\left\lvert\beta-\beta^{\mathrm{(RWN)}}_{M}\right\rvert\leq 8\beta^{\mathrm{(RWN)}}_{M}p(r)^{-1}\left\lvert\nu-\nu(\infty)+\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2r}\right\rvert.

To bound the term in absolute-value signs on the right, we go back to the
\nu
equation in (
4.41
) and integrate it from
\infty
to find

\nu(r)=\nu(\infty)-\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2}\int_{r}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1-\chi^{2}}{s^{2}}-\frac{(s-2\nu)(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{s^{3}}\right)\ \mathrm{d}s=\nu(\infty)-\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2r}+\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2}\int_{r}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\chi^{2}}{s^{2}}+\frac{(s-2\nu)(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{s^{3}}\right)\ \mathrm{d}s.

In particular

\displaystyle\left\lvert\nu(r)-\nu(\infty)+\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2r}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2}\int_{r}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\chi^{2}}{s^{2}}+\frac{(s-2\nu)(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{s^{3}}\right)\ \mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2}\int_{r}^{\infty}\left(W_{3}^{2}s^{-2}q(s)^{3+2\mathsf{h}}e^{-2s}+\frac{3W_{4}^{2}}{4}sp(s)^{-1}q(s)^{1+2\mathsf{h}}e^{-2s}\right)\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2}\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-2}q(s)^{3+2\mathsf{h}}e^{-2s}\left(W_{3}^{2}+\frac{3W_{4}^{2}}{4}s^{3}p(s)^{-1}q(s)^{-2}\right)\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2}\left(W_{3}^{2}+\frac{3W_{4}^{2}}{32}\right)\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-2}q(s)^{3+2\mathsf{h}}e^{-2s}\ \mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathsf{g}}{2}\left(W_{3}^{2}+\frac{3W_{4}^{2}}{32}\right)K_{8}r^{-1}q(r)^{2+2\mathsf{h}}e^{-2r}.

Applying this to the above estimate (
4.174
), we have proved (
4.172
) for the constant
C_{2}=4K_{8}(W_{3}^{2}+3W_{4}^{2}/32)
.

Final remarks

In this paper, we initiated a potential study of the equations of motion for general-relativistic point charges under the classical, generalized theory of electromagnetism of BLTP, by rigorously proving that the single-particle, static case admits spacetime solutions with a finite electric-field energy and finite values for the electric potential and bare mass at the charge’s location. In this sense, it can interpreted as an analogue of the Reissner-Weyl-Nordström solutions, but without the latter’s pathological singularities. Our results provide a rigorous framework for embedding static point charges governed by BLTP electrodynamics into general relativity, setting the stage for future efforts to treat multi-particle systems and dynamic evolutions within this setting.

The author would like to thank his PhD advisors Michael Kiessling and Shadi Tahvildar-Zadeh for all the valuable discussions related to this project throughout his course of studies at Rutgers University.

Appendix

4.2.
The electric potential in GR

Here we provide justification for equation (
3.26
) relating the general-relativistic electric potential
\varphi
and the Faraday tensor
F
in the form
F_{tr}=e^{\alpha}\varphi^{\prime}(r)
, where
e^{\alpha}=\sqrt{-g_{tt}g_{rr}}
. The idea is to invoke the Equivalence Principle to define the quantity
\varphi^{\prime}(r)
— we remark that it is the derivative
\varphi^{\prime}
instead of the electric potential
\varphi
itself that is accessible by an observer measuring the EM fields.

In SR, using spherical coordinates
(c\overline{t},\overline{r},\overline{\theta},\overline{\phi})
constructed from an inertial system
(c\overline{t},\overline{x}^{1},\overline{x}^{2},\overline{x}^{3})
, the tensor
F=\mathrm{d}A
(
2.30
) for
A=-\varphi(\overline{r})\ \mathrm{d}(c\overline{t})
(
2.31
) is expressed as

(A.1)

F=-\varphi^{\prime}(\overline{r})\ \mathrm{d}\overline{r}\wedge\mathrm{d}(c\overline{t})=\varphi^{\prime}(\overline{r})\ \mathrm{d}(c\overline{t})\wedge\mathrm{d}\overline{r}.

This implies that
\varphi^{\prime}(\overline{r})=F_{tr}
. Now consider our general-relativistic spacetime
\mathcal{M}
as in subsection
3.3
with its coordinate system
(ct,r,\theta,\varphi)
. Loosely speaking, the Equivalence Principle states that the local laws of physics in GR around a given spacetime event reduce to those of SR if one uses
normal coordinates
at this event, which by definition means a coordinate system under which the metric and its first-order derivatives coincide with those of the Minkowski metric at this event. Thus, given an event
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}
with coordinates
(ct_{0},r_{0},\theta_{0},\phi_{0})
, we shall assume that the quantity
(\mathrm{d}\varphi/\mathrm{d}r)(r_{0})
that we wish to relate to the components of
F
is given by
F_{\overline{t}\overline{r}}
, where
(c\overline{t},\overline{r},\overline{\theta},\overline{\phi})
is a system of normal coordinates around
x_{0}
for which the metric assumes the spherical form of the Minkowski metric. The tensor-transformation law for changing the components of
F
from our coordinates
(x^{\mu})=(ct,r,\theta,\phi)
into the coordinates
(c\overline{t},\overline{r},\overline{\theta},\overline{\phi})
gives a workable expression for this quantity:

(A.2)

F_{\overline{t}\overline{r}}=\left(\frac{\partial x^{\zeta}}{\partial\overline{t}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial x^{\eta}}{\partial\overline{r}}\right)F_{\zeta\eta}=\left(\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{t}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{r}}\right)F_{tr}+\left(\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{t}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{r}}\right)F_{rt}=\left(\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{t}}\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{r}}-\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{t}}\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{r}}\right)F_{tr}.

(Let us assume
c=1
here for brevity). Now suppose, if only for simplicity, that our coordinate change acts as
(t,r)\longmapsto(\overline{t},\overline{r})
, leaving the spherical angles
(\theta,\phi)
untouched. Thus only the radial terms of the metric (
3.21
) transform into their corresponding terms of the spherical Minkowski metric:

(A.3)

g_{tt}(r)\ \mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}t+g_{rr}(r)\ \mathrm{d}r\otimes\mathrm{d}r=-\mathrm{d}\overline{t}\otimes\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}r\otimes\mathrm{d}\overline{r}.

This equality and all the upcoming ones are meant only to first order in
(\overline{t}-t_{0},\overline{r}-r_{0})
, which poses no problem for us because we wish to evaluate (
A.2
) only at
(ct_{0},r_{0},\theta_{0},\phi_{0})
anyway. Using

(A.4)

\mathrm{d}t=\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{t}}\mathrm{d}\overline{t}+\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{r}}\mathrm{d}\overline{r},\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{d}r=\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{t}}\mathrm{d}\overline{t}+\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{r}}\mathrm{d}\overline{r},

the left side of (
A.3
) evaluates to

\left(g_{tt}\left(\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{t}}\right)^{2}+g_{rr}\left(\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{t}}\right)^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\overline{t}\otimes\mathrm{d}\overline{t}+\left(g_{tt}\left(\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{r}}\right)^{2}+g_{rr}\left(\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{r}}\right)^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\overline{r}\otimes\mathrm{d}\overline{r}\\
+\left(g_{tt}\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{t}}\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{r}}+g_{rr}\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{t}}\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{r}}\right)\big(\mathrm{d}\overline{t}\otimes\mathrm{d}\overline{r}+\mathrm{d}\overline{r}\otimes\mathrm{d}\overline{t}\big).

Therefore (
A.3
) yields the three equations
-x^{2}+y^{2}=-1
,
-z^{2}+w^{2}=1
and
-xz+yw=0
for the auxiliary quantities

x=\sqrt{\left\lvert g_{tt}(r_{0})\right\rvert}\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{t}}\bigg|_{(t_{0},r_{0})},\ y=\sqrt{g_{rr}(r_{0})}\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{t}}\bigg|_{(t_{0},r_{0})},\ z=\sqrt{\left\lvert g_{tt}(r_{0})\right\rvert}\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{r}}\bigg|_{(t_{0},r_{0})},\ w=\sqrt{g_{rr}(r_{0})}\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{r}}\bigg|_{(t_{0},r_{0})}.

In particular note that

(xw-yz)^{2}=(xw-yz)^{2}-(-xz+yw)^{2}=(x^{2}-y^{2})(-z^{2}+w^{2})=(1)(1)=1\hskip 9.24994pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptxw-yz=1,

where, when taking the square root in the end, we also assumed that the transformation
(t,r)\longmapsto(\overline{t},\overline{r})
must preserve orientation. Thus the quantity (
A.2
) that expresses our desired
\varphi^{\prime}(r)
turns into

(A.5)

F_{\overline{t}\overline{r}}=\left(\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{t}}\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{r}}-\frac{\partial r}{\partial\overline{t}}\frac{\partial t}{\partial\overline{r}}\right)F_{tr}=\left(\left\lvert g_{tt}(r_{0})\right\rvert^{-1/2}xg_{rr}(r_{0})^{-1/2}w-g_{rr}(r_{0})^{-1/2}y\left\lvert g_{tt}(r_{0})\right\rvert^{-1/2}z\right)F_{tr}=\left\lvert g_{tt}(r_{0})g_{rr}(r_{0})\right\rvert^{-1/2}F_{tr}.

Using the form (
3.21
) of our metric, the final result is precisely
e^{-\alpha}F_{tr}
. This motivates our definition
\varphi^{\prime}=e^{-\alpha}F_{tr}
in the GR setting.

4.3.
The E-M-BLTP system for a static particle

Here we derive the E-M-BLTP system (
3.30
) as stated in Proposition (
3.2
).

Our metric (
3.21
) has the general form of a static, spherically symmetric metric

(A.6)

g=-e^{2\Phi(r)}\ \mathrm{d}(ct)\otimes\mathrm{d}(ct)+e^{2\Lambda(r)}\ \mathrm{d}r\otimes\mathrm{d}r+r^{2}\ \mathrm{d}\theta\otimes\mathrm{d}\theta+r^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\ \mathrm{d}\phi\otimes\mathrm{d}\phi

for
e^{2\Phi}=\beta^{-1}e^{2\alpha}
and
e^{2\Lambda}=\beta
. Thus the non-vanishing metric and inverse-metric components are

(A.7)

\begin{array}[]{lclclcl}g_{tt}=-\beta^{-1}e^{2\alpha},&&g_{rr}=\beta,&&g_{\theta\theta}=r^{2},&&g_{\phi\phi}=r^{2}\sin^{2}\theta,\\
g^{tt}=-\beta e^{-2\alpha},&&g^{rr}=\beta^{-1},&&g^{\theta\theta}=r^{-2},&&g^{\phi\phi}=r^{-2}(\sin\theta)^{-2},\\
\end{array}

and in particular

(A.8)

\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}=r^{2}e^{\alpha}\sin\theta.

The Einstein tensor
(G_{\mu\nu})
corresponding to (
A.6
) turns out (see
[
14
]
, p. 260) to be diagonal and given by

(A.9)

\displaystyle G_{tt}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{2\Phi}}{r^{2}}\left(r(1-e^{-2\Lambda})\right)^{\prime},

(A.10)

\displaystyle G_{rr}

\displaystyle=-\frac{e^{2\Lambda}}{r^{2}}(1-e^{-2\Lambda})+\frac{2\Phi^{\prime}}{r},

\displaystyle G_{\theta\theta}

\displaystyle=r^{2}e^{-2\Lambda}\left(\Phi^{\prime\prime}+(\Phi^{\prime})^{2}-\Phi^{\prime}\Lambda^{\prime}+\frac{\Phi^{\prime}-\Lambda^{\prime}}{r}\right),

\displaystyle G_{\phi\phi}

\displaystyle=G_{\theta\theta}\sin^{2}\theta.

The first two of these components are the ones we will need later. Written in terms of
\alpha=\Lambda+\Phi
and
\beta=e^{2\Lambda}
, they become

(A.11)

G_{tt}=\frac{e^{2\alpha}}{r\beta^{2}}\left(\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}+\frac{\beta}{r}-\frac{1}{r}\right),\hskip 9.24994ptG_{rr}=\frac{1}{r}\left(-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}-\frac{\beta}{r}+\frac{1}{r}+2\alpha^{\prime}\right).

The 2-form
F
is given in terms of the electric-deviation scalar
\chi
as in (
3.29
). The equation
\mathrm{d}F=0
comes for free using this expression. Two of the other equations defining our spacetime are the vacuum law (
2.27
) and Maxwell’s equation (
2.26
) for
M
, repeated here:

(A.12)

M={\star}(F+\varkappa^{-2}\mathrm{d}\delta F),\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{d}M=0.

Note that the
M
equation above is homogeneous now because the 4-current
J
is identically null on
\mathcal{M}
— the point charge’s worldline is not considered part of the manifold. The remaining equations are the EFE (Einstein Field Equations)

(A.13)

G_{\mu\nu}:=R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}Rg_{\mu\nu}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu},\hskip 9.24994pt\mu,\nu=t,r,\theta,\phi,

where the Hilbert stress-energy tensor
(T_{\mu\nu})
is defined in (
3.3
). For future use, we compute the Hodge duals of
\mathrm{d}(ct)
and
\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r
. According to definition (
1.5
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge{\star}\mathrm{d}(ct)=\big\langle\mathrm{d}(ct),\mathrm{d}(ct)\big\rangle_{g}\ \sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}\ \mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi,

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r\big)\wedge{\star}\big(\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r\big)=\big\langle\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r\big\rangle_{g}\ \sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}\ \mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi,

while definition (
1.3
) gives

\big\langle\mathrm{d}(ct),\mathrm{d}(ct)\big\rangle_{g}=g^{tt},\hskip 9.24994pt\big\langle\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r\big\rangle_{g}=\det\left(\begin{array}[]{rr}g^{tt}&g^{tr}\\
g^{rt}&g^{rr}\end{array}\right)=g^{tt}g^{rr}.

Then, using (
A.7
) and (
A.8
), we quickly deduce

(A.14)

\displaystyle{\star}\mathrm{d}(ct)

\displaystyle=-r^{2}\beta e^{-\alpha}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi,

(A.15)

\displaystyle{\star}\big(\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r\big)

\displaystyle=-r^{2}e^{-\alpha}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi.

As is well known, the EFE do not form a completely independent system of equations. We leave to the end of this section a quick check that the complete information for our spacetime is given by the Maxwell equations (
A.12
) together with the
(t,t)
and
(r,r)
equations of the EFE (
A.13
). Now we proceed to write out these equations in the
(t,r,\theta,\phi)
coordinates. For this we need to note down the components of
F
,
\delta F
and
\mathrm{d}\delta F
. The only nonzero components of
F
are

F_{tr}=Qr^{-2}e^{\alpha}(\chi-1),\hskip 9.24994ptF_{rt}=-Qr^{-2}e^{\alpha}(\chi-1).

Since the metric is diagonal, raising an index
\zeta
of a tensor component amounts to multiplying it by
g^{\zeta\zeta}
(no sum in
\zeta
is implied):
\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1897pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.56407pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\eta}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1897pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.56407pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\eta}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1897pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.56407pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\eta}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1897pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.56407pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\eta}}}}=\sum_{\mu}g^{\zeta\mu}F_{\mu\eta}=g^{\zeta\zeta}F_{\zeta\eta}+\sum_{\mu\neq\zeta}g^{\zeta\mu}F_{\mu\eta}=g^{\zeta\zeta}F_{\zeta\eta}
. Therefore, the only nonzero components of
(\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1897pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.56407pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\eta}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1897pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.56407pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\eta}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1897pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.56407pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\eta}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.05705pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1897pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.56407pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\eta}}}})
are

\mathchoice{F^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}=-Qr^{-2}\beta e^{-\alpha}(\chi-1),\hskip 9.24994pt\mathchoice{F^{{{r}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{r}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{r}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{r}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}=-Qr^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}(\chi-1).

Raising the second index, we also find the only nonzero components of
(F^{\zeta\eta})
:

F^{tr}=-Qr^{-2}e^{-\alpha}(\chi-1),\hskip 9.24994ptF^{rt}=Qr^{-2}e^{-\alpha}(\chi-1).

Next we compute

(\delta F)^{\mu}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}}\partial_{\lambda}\big(\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}F^{\lambda\mu}\big)=-r^{-2}(\sin\theta)^{-1}e^{-\alpha}\partial_{\lambda}\big(r^{2}e^{\alpha}\sin\theta F^{\lambda\mu}\big),\hskip 9.24994pt\mu=t,r,\theta,\phi.

The expression being differentiated here only depends on the variables
r
and
\theta
, but for
\lambda=\theta
the term
F^{\lambda\mu}
disappears. Hence only
\lambda=r
must be considered:
(\delta F)^{\mu}=-r^{-2}e^{-\alpha}\big(r^{2}e^{\alpha}F^{r\mu}\big)^{\prime}
, which shows that
(\delta F)^{\mu}
is only nonzero when
\mu=t
, in which case it is given by

(\delta F)^{t}=-r^{-2}e^{-\alpha}\big(r^{2}e^{\alpha}F^{rt}\big)^{\prime}=-Qr^{-2}e^{-\alpha}\chi^{\prime}.

Lowering its index, we find the 1-form
\delta F=(\delta F)_{t}\ \mathrm{d}(ct)
for

(\delta F)_{t}=Qr^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime}.

We will also need
\mathrm{d}\delta F
, which is given by
\big((\delta F)_{t}\big)^{\prime}\ \mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}(ct)=(\mathrm{d}\delta F)_{tr}\ \mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r
for

(\mathrm{d}\delta F)_{tr}=-\big((\delta F)_{t}\big)^{\prime}=-Q\big(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime}\big)^{\prime}.

We can finally write out
M
from (
A.12
):

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle={\star}(F+\varkappa^{-2}\mathrm{d}\delta F)

\displaystyle={\star}\big(Qr^{-2}e^{\alpha}(\chi-1)-Q\varkappa^{-2}(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime})^{\prime}\big)\ \mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=Q\big(r^{-2}e^{\alpha}(\chi-1)-Q\varkappa^{-2}(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime})^{\prime}\big)\big(-r^{2}e^{-\alpha}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi\big)

(A.16)

\displaystyle=Q\sin\theta\bigg(-(\chi-1)+\varkappa^{-2}r^{2}e^{-\alpha}(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime})^{\prime}\bigg)\ \mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi,

where we used equation (
A.15
) for
{\star}\big(\mathrm{d}(ct)\wedge\mathrm{d}r\big)
. Therefore, in the equation

0=\mathrm{d}M=Q\sin\theta\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\bigg(-(\chi-1)+\varkappa^{-2}r^{2}e^{-\alpha}(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime})^{\prime}\bigg)\ \mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi,

the term in parentheses must be equal to some constant
q
, giving us
M=Qq\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi
and

(A.17)

\frac{(\chi-1+q)e^{\alpha}}{r^{2}}=\varkappa^{-2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\frac{\chi^{\prime}e^{\alpha}}{r^{2}\beta}\right).

Finally, it turns out that
q=1
must be chosen in order for the charge at the singularity to be
Q
. This choice proves the
\chi
equation as claimed in (
3.30
). To see why
q=1
, we simply need to figure out how
Q
is related to
M
:

Proposition 4.8
.

The charge at the singularity is given by

Q=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\partial B_{R}(0)}M,

where
R>0
is arbitrary and
\partial B_{R}(0)
denotes the boundary of the 3-dimensional sphere

B_{R}(0):=\big\{ct_{0}\big\}\times\big\{(r,\theta,\phi):\,r\in[0,R),\ \theta\in[0,\pi],\ \phi\in[0,2\pi)\big\}

for an arbitrary instant
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. This implies that the value of
q
in
(
A.17
)
is
q=1
.

Proof
. In GR, the charge-density scalar field
\rho
associated with a coordinate system like ours is defined in terms of the four-current
vector

J=(J^{\mu})
by the relation

(A.18)

\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}J^{t}=c\rho.

Note that this holds for an inertial coordinate system in SR, where we had the 1-form
J=-c\rho\ \mathrm{d}(ct)
(equation (
2.22
) with
\bm{j}=0
) and thus, raising the index,
J^{t}=c\rho
. The reason why (
A.18
) is the correct generalization of this definition for GR has to do with the property of charge conservation (
2.2
) that must be satisfied by
\rho
and
\bm{j}
: Equation (
2.28
), namely
\delta J=0
, can only be written as a continuity equation (that is, an equation stating that the sum of
partial derivatives
of scalar quantities is zero) if one uses (
1.9
):

0=\delta J=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}}\partial_{\lambda}\big(\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}J^{\lambda}\big).

For this reason, the scalars
\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}J^{\lambda}
are identified with charge and current densities in GR. In particular, using the metric (
3.21
), the 1-form
J
is found by lowering the index from
J^{t}
in (
A.18
):

(A.19)

J=-\frac{e^{2\alpha}}{\beta}\frac{c\rho}{\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}}\ \mathrm{d}(ct).

Of course, we have said before that
J=0
, and this remains true on
\mathcal{M}
according to this formula, due to
\rho=Q\delta_{\bm{0}}
being a
\delta
distribution localized at the singularity. However, we now need to take into account the value of
J
at the singularity because we are about to perform a volume integral of
{\star}J
over a ball centered at
r=0
, so we must use the more precise equation (
A.19
). With it, the computation of the Hodge dual of
J
, using equation (
A.14
) for
{\star}\mathrm{d}(ct)
, reveals

(A.20)

{\star}J=c\rho\ \mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi

and thus
\int_{B_{R}(0)}{\star}J=c\,Q
. Now (
A.20
) and the
M
equation (
A.12
), namely
\mathrm{d}M=(4\pi/c){\star}J
, give us the claimed relation between
Q
and
M
:

\frac{4\pi}{c}c\,Q=\frac{4\pi}{c}\int_{B_{R}(0)}{\star}J=\int_{B_{R}(0)}\mathrm{d}M=\int_{\partial B_{R}(0)}M,

where Stokes’ theorem was used. To further simplify this, we use the expression
M=Qq\,\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi
(as mentioned just before the statement of this proposition), valid globally on the region where
r>0
and particularly also along
\partial B_{R}(0)
, to arrive at

4\pi Q=Qq\int_{\partial B_{R}(0)}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi=Qq\int_{0}^{2\pi}\hskip-6.47491pt\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\phi=4\pi Qq.

Hence
q=1
. ∎

Next we expand the relevant equations of the EFE. Using the expressions for the components of
F
,
\delta F
and
\mathrm{d}\delta F
found above, the invariants of
F
featuring in formula (
3.3
) for
T_{\mu\nu}
are determined to be

\displaystyle F_{\zeta\eta}F^{\zeta\eta}=2F_{tr}F^{tr}=-2Q^{2}r^{-4}(\chi-1)^{2},

\displaystyle(\delta F)_{\zeta}(\delta F)^{\zeta}=(\delta F)_{t}(\delta F)^{t}=-Q^{2}r^{-4}\beta^{-1}(\chi^{\prime})^{2},

\displaystyle F_{\zeta\eta}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)^{\zeta\eta}=2F^{tr}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)_{tr}=2Q^{2}r^{-2}e^{-\alpha}(\chi-1)(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime})^{\prime}.

Then we quickly calculate

\displaystyle\phantom{--}\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}F_{\zeta t}=Q^{2}r^{-4}\beta^{-1}e^{2\alpha}(\chi-1)^{2},

\displaystyle\phantom{--}\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}F_{\zeta r}=-Q^{2}r^{-4}\beta(\chi-1)^{2},

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{tt}}{4}F_{\zeta\eta}F^{\zeta\eta}=-\frac{Q^{2}}{2}r^{-4}\beta^{-1}e^{2\alpha}(\chi-1)^{2},

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{rr}}{4}F_{\zeta\eta}F^{\zeta\eta}=\frac{Q^{2}}{2}r^{-4}\beta(\chi-1)^{2},

\displaystyle\phantom{--}\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)_{\zeta t}=-Q^{2}r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}(\chi-1)\big(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime}\big)^{\prime},

\displaystyle\phantom{--}\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)_{\zeta r}=Q^{2}r^{-2}\beta e^{-\alpha}(\chi-1)\big(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime}\big)^{\prime},

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{tt}}{2}F_{\zeta\eta}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)^{\zeta\eta}=Q^{2}r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}(\chi-1)\big(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime}\big)^{\prime},

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{rr}}{2}F_{\zeta\eta}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)^{\zeta\eta}=-Q^{2}r^{-2}\beta e^{-\alpha}(\chi-1)\big(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime}\big)^{\prime},

\displaystyle-(\delta F)_{t}(\delta F)_{t}=-Q^{2}r^{-4}\beta^{-2}e^{2\alpha}(\chi^{\prime})^{2},

\displaystyle-(\delta F)_{r}(\delta F)_{r}=0,

\displaystyle\phantom{--}\frac{g_{tt}}{2}(\delta F)_{\zeta}(\delta F)^{\zeta}=\frac{Q^{2}}{2}r^{-4}\beta^{-2}e^{2\alpha}(\chi^{\prime})^{2},

\displaystyle\phantom{--}\frac{g_{rr}}{2}(\delta F)_{\zeta}(\delta F)^{\zeta}=-\frac{Q^{2}}{2}r^{-4}(\chi^{\prime})^{2},

with which we put together the tensor components

T_{\mu\nu}=:T^{\mathrm{(M)}}_{\mu\nu}+\varkappa^{-2}T^{\mathrm{(BLTP)}}_{\mu\nu},\hskip 9.24994pt\mu=\nu=t\text{ and }\mu=\nu=r,

piece by piece from definition (
3.3
) as follows:

\displaystyle 4\pi T^{\mathrm{(M)}}_{tt}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}F_{\zeta t}-\frac{g_{tt}}{4}F_{\zeta\eta}F^{\zeta\eta}=\frac{Q^{2}}{2}r^{-4}\beta^{-1}e^{2\alpha}(\chi-1)^{2},

\displaystyle 4\pi T^{\mathrm{(M)}}_{rr}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}F_{\zeta r}-\frac{g_{rr}}{4}F_{\zeta\eta}F^{\zeta\eta}=-\frac{Q^{2}}{2}r^{-4}\beta(\chi-1)^{2},

\displaystyle 4\pi T^{\mathrm{(BLTP)}}_{tt}

\displaystyle=2\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.00415pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.5925pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.1375pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)_{\zeta t}-\frac{g_{tt}}{2}F_{\zeta\eta}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)^{\zeta\eta}-(\delta F)_{t}(\delta F)_{t}+\frac{g_{tt}}{2}(\delta F)_{\zeta}(\delta F)^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=-Q^{2}r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}(\chi-1)\big(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime}\big)^{\prime}-\frac{Q^{2}}{2}r^{-4}\beta^{-2}e^{2\alpha}(\chi^{\prime})^{2},

\displaystyle 4\pi T^{\mathrm{(BLTP)}}_{rr}

\displaystyle=2\mathchoice{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}{F^{{{\zeta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.85727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1121pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.60207pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.92938pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.37813pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r}}}}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)_{\zeta r}-\frac{g_{rr}}{2}F_{\zeta\eta}(\mathrm{d}\delta F)^{\zeta\eta}-(\delta F)_{r}(\delta F)_{r}+\frac{g_{rr}}{2}(\delta F)_{\zeta}(\delta F)^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=Q^{2}r^{-2}\beta e^{-\alpha}(\chi-1)\big(r^{-2}\beta^{-1}e^{\alpha}\chi^{\prime}\big)^{\prime}-\frac{Q^{2}}{2}r^{-4}(\chi^{\prime})^{2}.

This gives

(A.21)

\displaystyle T_{tt}

\displaystyle=\frac{Q^{2}}{8\pi}\left(\frac{e^{2\alpha}(\chi-1)^{2}}{r^{4}\beta}-2\varkappa^{-2}\frac{e^{\alpha}(\chi-1)}{r^{2}\beta}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\frac{\chi^{\prime}e^{\alpha}}{r^{2}\beta}\right)-\varkappa^{-2}\frac{e^{2\alpha}(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{r^{4}\beta^{2}}\right),

(A.22)

\displaystyle T_{rr}

\displaystyle=\frac{Q^{2}}{8\pi}\left(-\frac{\beta(\chi-1)^{2}}{r^{4}}+2\varkappa^{-2}\frac{\beta e^{-\alpha}(\chi-1)}{r^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}r}\left(\frac{\chi^{\prime}e^{\alpha}}{r^{2}\beta}\right)-\varkappa^{-2}\frac{(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{r^{4}}\right).

Now using (
A.11
) and also using the
\chi
equation (
A.17
) to simplify the
\mathrm{d}/(\mathrm{d}r)
terms on the right sides of (
A.21
) and (
A.22
), the EFE (
A.13
) for indices
(t,t)
and
(r,r)
become

(A.23)

\displaystyle\frac{e^{2\alpha}}{r\beta^{2}}\left(\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}+\frac{\beta}{r}-\frac{1}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{GQ^{2}}{c^{4}}\left(\frac{e^{2\alpha}(1-\chi^{2})}{r^{4}\beta}-\varkappa^{-2}\frac{e^{2\alpha}(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{r^{4}\beta^{2}}\right),

(A.24)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\left(-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}-\frac{\beta}{r}+\frac{1}{r}+2\alpha^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{GQ^{2}}{c^{4}}\left(-\frac{\beta(1-\chi^{2})}{r^{4}}-\varkappa^{-2}\frac{(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{r^{4}}\right).

From here we see that the combination
(r/2)\big(\beta^{2}e^{-2\alpha}
(
A.23
)
+
(
A.24
)
\big)
produces the
\alpha
equation as claimed in the E-M-BLTP system (
3.30
), while (
A.23
) alone yields the
\beta
equation after trivial simplifications.

It remains to check our previous claim that the other Einstein equations do not contribute any new information to the problem. We check that the
(t,t)
– and the
(r,r)
-EFE combined with the identity
\nabla^{\nu}T_{\mu\nu}=0
(which holds independently from the EFE; see remarks right after equation (
3.2
)) imply all the other
(\mu,\nu)
-EFE:

Proposition 4.9
.

Equations
(
A.13
)
for
\mu=\nu=t
and
\mu=\nu=r
together imply the same equations for all other choices of the indices
\mu,\nu
.

Proof.

Given the diagonality of the metric and the simple form of the tensor
F
, one quickly checks that
(T_{\mu\nu})
is diagonal just like
(G_{\mu\nu})
. Thus, only the equations corresponding to
\mu=\nu=\theta
and
\mu=\nu=\phi
need to be checked to be superfluous. Moreover, the relation
T_{\phi\phi}=T_{\theta\theta}\sin^{2}\theta
is easy to derive from the definition of
(T_{\mu\nu})
, and, given that
(G_{\mu\nu})
also satisfies it, we see that the
(\phi,\phi)
equation in (
A.13
) is superfluous, as it is a multiple of the
(\theta,\theta)
equation. It is more convenient now to work with the
contravariant
tensors
(G^{\zeta\eta})
and
(T^{\zeta\eta})
. Given that the metric (
A.6
) is diagonal, these tensors are also diagonal and verify

(A.25)

G^{\phi\phi}=\frac{G^{\theta\theta}}{\sin^{2}\theta},\hskip 9.24994ptT^{\phi\phi}=\frac{T^{\theta\theta}}{\sin^{2}\theta}.

The divergence freeness of
(T_{\mu\nu})
is now written in the form
\nabla_{\nu}T^{\mu\nu}=0
, which is expanded in components as

(A.26)

\partial_{\nu}T^{\mu\nu}+\Gamma^{\mu}_{\nu\xi}T^{\xi\nu}+\Gamma^{\nu}_{\nu\xi}T^{\mu\xi}=0,\hskip 9.24994pt\mu=t,r,\theta,\phi.

The Einstein tensor is also identically divergence-free, that is, independently from the EFE:

(A.27)

\partial_{\nu}G^{\mu\nu}+\Gamma^{\mu}_{\nu\xi}G^{\xi\nu}+\Gamma^{\nu}_{\nu\xi}G^{\mu\xi}=0,\hskip 9.24994pt\mu=t,r,\theta,\phi.

Multiplying (
A.26
) by
8\pi G/c^{4}
, then subtracting its
\mu=r
version from that of (
A.27
), we get

(A.28)

\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\nu}}\left(G^{r\nu}-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T^{r\nu}\right)+\Gamma^{r}_{\nu\xi}\left(G^{\xi\nu}-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T^{\xi\nu}\right)+\Gamma^{\nu}_{\nu\xi}\left(G^{r\xi}-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T^{r\xi}\right)=0.

In the first term between parentheses here, only
\nu=r
can contribute anything, because
(G^{\zeta\eta})
and
(T^{\zeta\eta})
are diagonal, but the
\nu=r
case also vanishes due to (
A.13
) having been assumed for
(\mu,\nu)=(r,r)
. For the same reasons (also using the
(\mu,\nu)=(t,t)
equation), the third term of (
A.28
) vanishes, while in the second one only the values
\xi=\nu=\theta
and
\xi=\nu=\phi
can make nonzero contributions. Thus (
A.28
) reduces to

0=\Gamma^{r}_{\theta\theta}\left(G^{\theta\theta}-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T^{\theta\theta}\right)+\Gamma^{r}_{\phi\phi}\left(G^{\phi\phi}-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T^{\phi\phi}\right)=\left(\Gamma^{r}_{\theta\theta}+\frac{\Gamma^{r}_{\phi\phi}}{\sin^{2}\theta}\right)\left(G^{\theta\theta}-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T^{\theta\theta}\right)\\
=-2r\beta^{-1}\left(G^{\theta\theta}-\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T^{\theta\theta}\right),

where (
A.25
) was used and the Christoffel symbols
\Gamma^{r}_{\theta\theta}=-r\beta^{-1}
and
\Gamma^{r}_{\phi\phi}=-r\beta^{-1}\sin^{2}\theta
were calculated from their definition (
1.2
). Since
r\beta^{-1}
does not vanish, the
(\theta,\theta)
-EFE is established.
∎

4.4.
The field-energy formula

Here we show a derivation of the integrand in (
3.32
) for the electric-field energy
\mathcal{E}
written as an expression of the unknowns
\alpha,\beta,\chi
. For the 1-form
P
defined in (
3.31
) in terms of the vector field
X=\partial_{t}
, we calculate

P_{\mu}=T_{\mu\nu}X^{\nu}=T_{\mu\nu}\delta^{\nu}_{t}=T_{\mu t}=T_{tt}\delta_{\mu}^{t},

where we used the fact that
(T_{\mu\nu})
is diagonal. This gives

P=P_{t}\ \mathrm{d}(ct)\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for }P_{t}=T_{tt}.

The Hodge dual
{\star}P
is calculated with the help of (
A.14
):

(A.29)

{\star}P=T_{tt}\big(g^{tt}\sqrt{\left\lvert g\right\rvert}\ \mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi\big)=-T_{tt}r^{2}\beta e^{-\alpha}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi.

Plugging this into (
3.32
) and using the expression for
T_{tt}
that can be read off (
A.23
), we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\{t=t_{0}\}}{\star}P

\displaystyle=\frac{Q^{2}}{8\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}\beta e^{-\alpha}\sin\theta\left(\frac{e^{2\alpha}(1-\chi^{2})}{r^{4}\beta}-\varkappa^{-2}\frac{e^{2\alpha}(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{r^{4}\beta^{2}}\right)\ \mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\phi

\displaystyle=\frac{Q^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{\alpha}}{r^{2}}\left(1-\chi^{2}-\varkappa^{-2}\frac{(\chi^{\prime})^{2}}{\beta}\right)\ \mathrm{d}r,

which is as claimed in (
3.32
). To justify why a minus sign appears in front of the integral in the definition (
3.32
) of
\mathcal{E}
, we have to check that this result generalizes that of SR, namely (
2.14
). This is immediate: Plugging (
A.29
) directly into the energy integral without explicitly writing out
T_{tt}
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=\int_{\{t=t_{0}\}}T_{tt}r^{2}\beta e^{-\alpha}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi

\displaystyle=\int_{\{t=t_{0}\}}(g_{tt})^{2}T^{tt}r^{2}\beta e^{-\alpha}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}r\wedge\mathrm{d}\theta\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{3\alpha}}{\beta}T^{tt}r^{2}\sin\theta\ \mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\phi

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{e^{3\alpha}}{\beta}T^{tt}\ \mathrm{d}^{3}\bm{x},

which reduces to (
2.14
) (since
\mathfrak{e}=T^{tt}
) when the Minkowski metric is used, that is, when
\alpha\equiv 0
and
\beta\equiv 1
.
